They are checked against the separate K-JK transcription and the actual embedded Arabic text in the Dubai scan, I:734–741. The modern editorial source note after the proverb in K-JK is excluded. 3. The contrast مِلْك / مُلْك is ownership / kingship. This order is confirmed by the scanned vocalization and Turkish comparison. The preference for malik is Zamakhsharī’s, not an editorial choice to suppress the mālik reading. 4. K001-U1: both transcriptions say “Raḥmān of this world and the next, and Raḥīm of this world.” The unexpected final “this world” is also supported by the Turkish text, p.76, and is retained; further Arabic scan collation is pending. Do not silently correct it. 5. K001-U2: the vowel-assimilation examples and minor poetic/proper-name vocalizations require a second Arabic scan pass. 6. Reports are translated as reports. No new independent judgment of authenticity is supplied. Ṭībī’s comments on the closing reports must be translated separately at the end of his Fātiḥa layer. 7. Still needed: complete line-by-line scan comparison of K001.01–22 against ʿUbaykān and the YEK facing Arabic; a second English fidelity pass; the rest of Ṭībī I:677–770. 8. Technical policy: iltifāt, badal, ḥāl, tawfīq, alṭāf, milk/mulk, and the actual Arabic reading variants are preserved where their distinctions carry the argument. Square-bracketed clarifications are editorial, not medieval source text. C04 TURKISH COMPARISON ADDENDUM The complete Turkish Fātiḥa text, even-numbered pages 66–98, paragraphs 9–78, was read against this draft. The English poem was corrected to news concerning Abū al-Aswad, rather than transmission on his authority. Nibz and allaha were corrected in the lexical discussion. Arabic remains controlling; Turkish expansions, editorial hadith judgments, and occasional interpretive differences have not been silently incorporated. The Turkish numbered verse scheme excludes the opening basmala from its displayed 1–7 sequence; Arabic reference numbers in this draft retain the usual basmala-counting scheme. T104-U2: XVI:574 prints وأقر بدء إحراق كل أحد على قدر استحقاقه. “The commencement of each person’s burning is fixed according to what he deserves” is a provisional construal of this difficult phrase. The scan confirms the wording; further witnesses are needed. T104-U3: The main text at XVI:576 reads يلزم, “would follow,” in its final sentence; the printed apparatus records a witness with لا يلزم, “would not follow.” This reverses the implication. The translation follows the printed main text and retains this unresolved variant explicitly; it does not silently attribute a settled doctrine of everlasting punishment for every slanderer to Ṭībī.
al-Fīl · source and review notes
TEXTUAL AND REVIEW NOTES K105-U1: The conflicting elephant counts and the clause “and it was alone” occur in the printed base at XVI:578 as well as the transcription. The English marks the alternative rather than inventing a single consistent count. T105-U1: The electronic wording says “with dotted ghayn” when explaining عرض, which begins with ʿayn. The intelligible word is rendered ʿurḍ; the letter-name needs scan/manuscript collation before finalisation. K105-U2: The DKA poem reads عدوا where Ṭībī’s gloss explicitly discusses غدوا, “soon/tomorrow”; the latter is followed and the difference recorded.
Quraysh · source and review notes
TEXTUAL AND REVIEW NOTES K106-U1 / T106-U1: The camel verse has zahw in the printed Dubai base and rahw in the DKA transcription. Both are preserved and explained. The difficult printed phrase الزهو غير الإدراك appears in the transcription as well; it is not securely established as a transcription error. The bracketed reconstruction of the cited verse and its relation to the adjacent lexical explanations require further fine collation before final publication. The provisional account above retains all the lexical alternatives rather than suppressing the difficulty. T106-U2: The reading “al-Zubayr, from al-Zajjāj” occurs in the print; the modern apparatus notes a witness omission. It remains attributed as printed, without asserting a resolved identification.
al-Māʿūn · source and review notes
REVIEW FLAGS T107-U1: The six-verse/Medinan heading differs from the base’s seven-verse alternatives. It is preserved as transmitted, not adopted as a settled verse count. T107-U2: The elaborate argument on ʿan/fī and legal responsibility is fully retained. Printed XVI:597 was inspected; further comparison of the quoted authorities is pending.
al-Kawthar · source and review notes
TEXTUAL AND REVIEW NOTES K108-U1: The first electronic Kashshāf witness prints أعطيناك despite immediately saying “with nūn.” The embedded Kashshāf in Ṭībī has أنطيناك, which supplies the intended dialectal form. T108-U1: The electronic text drops the oath and the Prophet’s reply in the al-Rubayyiʿ report. The full words above are restored from printed XVI:603, visually inspected. T108-U2: The printed phrase وأن المعطى لم يكن عظيما، إلا أن المعطي عظيم remains syntactically difficult. The provisional concessive rendering is retained with an explicit flag; do not silently rewrite it as an unqualified claim about the gift’s size. The following sentence expressly describes both gift and Giver as great. T108-U3: The phrase ليدمج معنى معطى قطع النفس in the scan/transcription is also awkward. “So as to incorporate the meaning of cutting the soul off” renders the evident relation of sacrifice to self-denial, but the extra معطى needs further textual collation. K108-U2: Jamʿ is Muzdalifa in the Arabic. The Turkish comparison’s phrasing places the dawn prayer at Minā; the English follows the Arabic distinction between Jamʿ and Minā.
al-Kāfirūn · source and review notes
SOURCE AND REVIEW NOTES T109-U1: The working transcription drops “before” in the sentence on p. 608 asserting worship according to a revealed law before the mission, and contains minor damaged forms in the surrounding quotations. The scan was inspected: قبل المبعث is present in print and confirms the restoration. The complete closing pages 611–612 were also visually checked. T109-U2: Ṭībī both preserves and criticizes al-Zamakhsharī's assertion about worship before the mission. The assertion has not been silently rewritten to agree with the criticism. Similarly, both positions on abrogation and all six explanations of the apparent repetition are retained. T109-U3: Punctuation distinguishing al-Rāzī's quoted argument from Ṭībī's interventions is editorial in this translation. The interventions explicitly introduced as “I say” remain attributed to Ṭībī.
al-Naṣr · source and review notes
TEXTUAL AND REVIEW NOTES T110-U1: The electronic text omits the quotation at 62:2; “and teaches them the Book and wisdom” is restored from printed XVI:615 (physical PDF page 614). K110-U1: The two-year survival report conflicts with the Farewell-Pilgrimage dating; Ṭībī’s chronological criticism is preserved separately. No historical reconciliation is imposed. K110-U2: Nafīr in the DKA electronic text is rendered following nafas in Ṭībī’s printed base and lexical explanation; the variant is retained here for further collation. T110-U2: The supplied volume XVI scan skips printed p. 613: physical page 612 shows printed 612, and physical page 613 shows printed 614. The chapter opening on printed p. 613 is present in the electronic transcription and is translated here; a replacement scan is still needed for its visual collation. Do not apply a single page offset throughout this PDF.
al-Masad · source and review notes
REVIEW FLAGS T111-U1: The transmitted camel verse has rāhiq; the modern apparatus records zāhiq. The author’s own gloss explains rāhiq, retained here. His attribution of the completion to al-Zajjāj is preserved, though the editor disputes it. T111-U2: The printed transition الأصهب before the al-Muṭliʿ quotation is awkward; no missing prose has been invented. The explanation of aṣhab is fully translated where it occurs.
al-Ikhlāṣ · source and review notes
END OF TRANSLATED AUTHORIAL TEXT Review: full draft; one complete scan pass over XVI 632–643; one Turkish base-text comparison over VI 1460–1467; no independent external review. Four substantive interpretive/source flags remain (T112-U1–U4). Subsequent passes must retain both authors' arguments and all quoted authorities, even where they conflict.
al-Falaq · source and review notes
END OF TRANSLATED AUTHORIAL TEXT Full first pass; one complete Arabic scan pass and one Turkish base-text comparison. Three unresolved readings remain. The dispute about sorcery is preserved as a dispute between the authors and their quoted authorities; it has not been harmonized.
al-Nās · source and review notes
END OF TRANSLATED AUTHORIAL TEXT Full first pass; one complete Arabic scan pass and one Turkish base-text comparison. Three principal interpretive/source flags remain, plus the “two classes/attributes” reading. The separate book epilogue is pending and is not part of this completion claim.

Sources and coverage

Working Arabic transcriptions and GNAMMA commentary translation. Ṭībī is complete for the 18 short sūrahs; al-Fātiḥa is limited to part 1, I:677–686. See the review notes for unresolved readings.
